Because it contains an indeterminate substance that has no clarity, when, according to whom, cannot be assessed, so it has caused serious injustices so far, from time to time, it has also caused loss of life. Therefore, the concept of reasonable doubt must be made especially clear and that everyone who reads it, knowingly approach it and decide accordingly, otherwise there may be very serious consequences, and there may be damage to society and people. A law that is to be done should be very clear and practical, not something that will cause people doubt or defy future negativity. It turns out that there are some very serious distances between them and party fans that people feel distant from. A concept that we often encounter during the social distance pandemic period, but the purpose of use here is that the questions of the party's supporters, which are most distant to different people, reveal the details: The hearts of 74.9% of the people involved in the study do not want to be neighbors; they do not want to marry the party's supporters; yet, 72 percent do not want to do business with one of the party's supporters; 66 percent do not want to allow their children to play with the children of their party; 68 percent do not want to be neighbors with someone who are not their own; 38 percent do not want to be their own; 38 percent, 99% of them do not want to be interested in their own parties; 38 percent, 99% of them do not want to be able to take on their own. One of the striking details of the study is the title of moral superiority. People think that the supporters of the party they feel close to are largely patriots, working for the country, honorable, open-minded, intelligent and generous, and there's an opposite to distant parties. In addition, they think they are hypocrites and a threat to Turkey. Another conclusion in the study is that the presidential systems are the systems that result in the loss of other candidates, which result in the election of 50+1 per cent of the vote-winning candidate, and that the will of the loser is not reflected in state administration in any way, and that the political climate is tensed. So it's considered by its nature as a system that increases polarization. The parliamentary systems allow political parties who want to establish government, legislature and control functions to form coalitions -- compromises -- so that they have the idea that they'll settle for polarization. We see polarisation in Turkey particularly revolves around political parties and political issues. While the economy has political extensions on issues such as violence against women, fighting the corona virus, and fear of earthquakes, people have a strong approach to each other in these matters. One of the most remarkable results of this detailed study is that politicians have the most impact on polarization; they contribute directly to polarization by their rhetoric, their aims, and their self-discrimination. The fact that politicians -- you, us -- prefer a more unifying language -- can eliminate polarization. So we, as members of any political party, see people as just a political opponent, not to be hostile to them, no matter what political party they belong to in our province, the market, or the market, hand in hand, side by side, in the mosque -- I don't know -- at the funeral, at the festival, on the shore -- this convergence, this reconciliation, and it's said that the world of the person, whatever the sun is, revolves around it. If our sun becomes tolerance, love, peace, our world revolves around it and we leave for future generations a peaceful, peaceful, mutually tolerant world. We, Mr. President-in-Chief, are intent on turning Turkey into a holiday place, and meeting the Turkish nation at the feast table with 83 million people. With this feeling and thought, I wish the new year to bring health, peace, success to our nation, and respect to all of you.
